# Song audit — Phase 16-a

Every song in the catalogue that a limit changed, in the order to rebuild it
(Phase 16, D81). Read-only: no song was changed to make this. The ledger
[`scripts/songs/audit.json`](../../../scripts/songs/audit.json) holds the same
rows with a `state` per song, which each 16-e batch ticks; this page is
written from it. The scan the audit started from is `npm run songs:audit`
(`scripts/songs/audit.ts`, its output `scripts/songs/audit-scan.json`, not
committed).

## How it was done (2026-11-01)

1. **The scan** read every song in `STYLES`: its sections, the bars and grid
   of each, distinct bars, slots, the Songsterr tabs its comment cites, the
   comment lines that say rounded, cut, left out, "N of M" and the like, and
   the day its key line went in (`git blame -M`, so the 2026-10-31 move of
   songs in 56dfb90f6 does not re-date them). 1,108 of the 1,217 songs were
   first built under a 32-bar cap.
2. **The reading**, as forks over eleven groups of drummers, one at a time,
   saving per drummer: each flagged song's comment read whole, with the
   drummer's `drumming-research.md` section and the batch notes in
   `drummer-batches.md`, and every song built under a 32-bar cap from a tab
   or written bars read whether flagged or not. Each hit was classed as the
   limits' doing or not; a song left generated only because of the cap, or
   never built for the limits, is on the list. A record split over several
   song keys is one row.
3. **The songs not built**: 69 of them, from the forks' notes and the
   batch write-ups, each with the tab already found where there is one.

## What it found

- **811 songs on the list**: 742 in the catalogue and bent, 69 left out. 276 hits read and cleared (no tab, a drumless bar, a machine tab, "left out" meaning another instrument, a stale research note).
- **About 18,919 bars** of records are not played as recorded in the songs to rebuild.
- **Priority**: 234 at 1 (much of the record missing, or the part drummers learn), 319 at 2, 258 at 3 (a few notes rounded, a bar short).
- **What the rebuilds wait on**: 641 need sections of any length (D76), 407 meter and grid per bar (D77), 42 a song bigger than a link (D79), 103 a limit outside Phase 16 (below). **37 can be rebuilt now**, needing no code.
- **4 drummers** have nothing on the list: Tito Puente, Max Roach, Jerry Allison, Tommy Ramone.

### D78: no song needs letters past Z

The most sections any song has is 26, and nine songs sit there: All the Small Things, Arabella, Disasterpiece, God Wants Us All to Work in Factories, Mirage, Panic Attack, Pull Me Under, The Mirror, Won't Get Fooled Again. Every one is at 26 because of the eight-bar cap or a section split off for its grid, not because its record has 26 parts: Arabella is about eleven parts of the record, Mirage's theme and solos are nine sections of eight. Kenny Clarke's Love Me or Leave Me and Two Not One each dropped two fours to stay inside 26; with D76 a four is one section. So **D78 is noted, not built**: the letters stay A–Z.

### The groove rules are already lifted

Commit 56dfb90f6 (2026-10-31, "Hold songs to the body, not the generator's taste") holds a written section to two hands and two feet at the song's tempo (`physical.ts`) and keeps the groove rules for grooves the generator invents; it allows any tempo ratio from ×¼ to ×4 and tempos to 380. So the losses filed `rule-backbeat`, `rule-run`, `rule-air`, `rule-first-bar`, `rule-crash`, `ratio-step` and the 190-cap `tempo-bent` need **no code**, only a rebuild: they stay in songs built before that commit. They are not in the Phase 16 limits table because they were lifted the day before it was written.

### Limits outside the Phase 16 table

103 rows name one. Each is for the owner to rule on before its rows can be built; until then they stay `open` with the limit in `blockedBy`.

| Limit                                 | What it stops                                                            | Example                                                               |
| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| No tempo change within a break        | An accelerando or a ritardando at the end                                | Try a Little Tenderness, Limelight, This Is a Call, Bat Out of Hell   |
| Meters the app lacks                  | 3/8, 5/8, 10/8, 13/8, 13/16, 15/16, 17/16, 18/16, 20/8, 31/32            | The Mirror (17/16, 13/8) and others                                   |
| No sixteenths and triplets in one bar | A bar whose figure mixes grids; **D77's per-bar grid does not fix this** | Boris, Love Spreads, ABC, Soft Winds, Let's Cool One                  |
| A figure row holds 32 steps           | A bar of 32nds, or a long bar, past 32 steps                             | Rosanna, Roundabout, Children of the Grave, Hot Fun in the Summertime |
| No kit change within a song           | Brushes to sticks                                                        | So What, All Blues                                                    |
| A grid neither sixteenth nor triplet  | Quintuplets, septuplets and feels between the grids                      | Tender, Rusty Cage, Roundabout                                        |

### Count per limit

A row can name several.

| Limit               | Rows | What it did                                                                                        |
| ------------------- | ---: | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `bars-cut`          |  421 | Bars of the record not played: a passage shortened to fit a section or the song                    |
| `repeats-collapsed` |  387 | A passage played as a few of its commonest bars, repeated, not bar for bar                         |
| `grid-rounded`      |  241 | A 32nd, sextuplet or triplet figure moved to the section's grid                                    |
| `rule-run`          |  205 | A run broken (into threes, or a stroke moved to a tom) by `RUN_RATE` or the critic's four-in-a-row |
| `meter-split`       |  180 | A bar in another meter merged, split off as its own section, or written in the wrong meter         |
| `rule-backbeat`     |  139 | A stop, hit or intro bar dropped or merged so the critic found a backbeat                          |
| `ending-dropped`    |  131 | The record's ending, outro or fade left out                                                        |
| `left-out`          |  116 | The song, or a whole part of it, not built; or left generated only because of the cap              |
| `intro-dropped`     |  109 | The record's intro left out                                                                        |
| `other-limit`       |  103 | A limit outside the Phase 16 table (below)                                                         |
| `tempo-bent`        |   57 | A tempo played faster or slower than the record (the 190 cap, or to fit a ratio)                   |
| `ratio-step`        |   52 | A section's tempo bent to one of the ratio's old allowed values                                    |
| `rule-air`          |   47 | A part dropped by the critic's air floor                                                           |
| `section-split`     |   45 | One part of the record split into sections of eight bars or fewer                                  |
| `note-moved`        |   37 | A note moved to another beat or drum to pass a rule                                                |
| `rule-first-bar`    |   30 | An opening dropped because the first bar must give the time                                        |
| `section-merged`    |    6 | Two parts of the record held as one section                                                        |
| `rule-crash`        |    6 | A crash moved by the crash-on-each-half rule                                                       |

## Before 16-e starts

- **Sources first** for the rows whose only tab is a machine one (Space Boogie, Erskine's live Weather Report songs and others marked in `notes`): a rebuild needs a better tab or a transcription, so those rows wait on research, not code.
- **Stale research**: the forks found `drumming-research.md` "Left out" lists that later rebuilds already fixed (Pull Me Under, The Mirror, Panic Attack, Crazy Little Thing Called Love, Around the World, Telephone, Go Your Own Way, Godzilla and others). 16-e corrects each as it passes; they are not rows.
- **In the Air Tonight** and Badge were cleared as kept to their tabs' commonest bars with no limit named; the former is worth a reading of its own.
